# Codex verification brief — relation map on the try-it page (commit bfcb83d)

You are an independent reviewer. Do NOT edit any tracked file. Write scratch files only under
`audit_evidence_claims/m1/`. Put your report in `audit_evidence_claims/m1/REPORT.md`.
End with exactly one line: `VERDICT: APPROVE` or `VERDICT: CHANGES REQUIRED`.

## What was added

`src/app.py` `Engine.explore(text, per_group=30)` and `POST /api/explore`; `web/try.html` section "0. 관계 지도"
(`drawMap`, `explore`, `itemDetail`, the GKO/TKO/ACT/PH tables, click-through and the "따라온 길" trail);
`tests/test_app.py::test_explore_draws_a_drug_and_refuses_a_biosimilar`. Run tests with
`python -m pytest tests -q -p no:cacheprovider` (317). Start the page with `python src/app.py [port]` (use a port
other than 8765, which may already be in use) and exercise it over HTTP.

The page is for a domain reviewer to look at one node (drug, target, company, investigator, trial, disease) and
everything a live edge ties it to, grouped by relation, and to click through. It is read-only.

## The guarantees claimed (page text in section 0 and the `explore` docstring)

1. Every neighbour shown is joined to the centre by a live (not retired) edge to a live node, and the group label
   states the relation and its direction correctly (e.g. "이 약의 임상을 주관한 곳 (주관 ≠ 개발)" for
   Company -SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF-> Drug seen from the drug).
2. Details shown for a neighbour are the stored values: trial phases/status/start/title/url, sponsor trial count
   (`evidence_trials`) and sponsor class, drug trial count in the graph (EVALUATES), max phase, action type.
3. A name the question linker refuses (biosimilar, distinct product, tracer) is reported as refused, never drawn
   as the originator molecule; an unknown name says "not found".
4. Groups are ordered by size and each list keeps the `per_group` neighbours with most evidence; the page says
   when a list is cut and shows the true total.
5. Nothing is written; no model is called.

## What to verify (reproduce; cite file:line and exact inputs)

A. **Wrong relations or directions.** Any centre where an edge is grouped under the wrong label or direction,
   self-loops (src == dst), edges with both ends equal to the centre, PARENT_OF/SPONSORS/STUDIES/ASSOCIATED_WITH
   direction wording, Investigator vs Company handling, nodes whose type the tables do not cover.
B. **Name resolution.** `explore` takes the first non-refused hit of `link_entities`. Inputs with several entities
   ("osimertinib EGFR"), a refused token next to a real one ("Mvasi bevacizumab"), salts vs parents, company aliases
   with `also_matching_ids` (only the first id is drawn — is that misleading?), node ids vs names that collide,
   case/whitespace/Unicode, long inputs. Is anything drawn that the input does not name, or a refused product drawn
   as the originator?
C. **Wrong values.** Trial counts, sponsor counts, max phase and action shown versus the database
   (`data/processed/oncokg.sqlite`); retired edges/nodes leaking in; counts computed over retired edges;
   the `trials` count chunking; JSON props that are null or malformed.
D. **Cutting and ordering.** Is the "most evidence" ordering what the page says (trials are ordered by start date
   string — is that what the text implies)? Does the map's subset (`drawMap` allocation) ever hide a whole group
   or misstate totals? The Disease node (thousands of neighbours) — performance and output size.
E. **Page safety and correctness.** HTML/SVG injection from labels, titles, ids, URLs (`href` with `javascript:`?),
   edge ids in `data-id` attributes, the trail/back buttons after errors, double clicks, refused/not-found states,
   dark mode legibility is not in scope.
F. **Server.** Error handling for empty/oversized input, concurrency with the lock, behaviour when the DB has
   retired rows.
G. **Docs and wording.** Section 0 text, README/README.ko lines for `app.py`, the module docstring: do they claim
   more than the code does? Korean labels in GKO/ACT/PH that misstate the stored code.

## Approval bar

No known way for the map to show a relation that is not a live edge, a wrong direction or relation wording, a
value that differs from the stored one, a refused product drawn as the originator, or script injection. Report
should-fix items (misleading wording, cut lists without notice) separately from blockers.

# M27 attack lane

Reviewed HEAD `9d8b7965f1168073c26b5914275749303c4342be`, previous query module `c42e89530084cd83073ad95090452d89acb4539a`. Two principal P1 findings breach the product-identity guarantee. The SN-38 forward input is a new regression; the complete affirmative focus phrase is a surviving mechanism from M26. No production patch was made.

## M27-B1: the named SN-38 payload is split before code recognition (P1, new regression)

Exact input: **`trastuzumab linked to SN-38`**. Current HEAD returns **CHEMBL1201585 / TRASTUZUMAB**, with the complete bare-trastuzumab map (**13 graph trials**) and the complete bare trial/sponsor QA evidence arrays (**12 / 12 rows**). The previous query module refuses this exact input. Changing only the payload spelling to **`trastuzumab linked to SN38`** refuses on both versions.

Source mechanism: `src/query.py:239-242` explicitly documents SN-38 but stores `sn38` in `_PAYLOAD_CODES`. `_alnum_words` (`src/query.py:112-114`) treats punctuation as separators. `_item_joined` (`src/query.py:255-261`) consequently tests `sn` and `38` individually, never `sn38`. Neither token is a reagent prefix or payload code. The `linked` branch at `src/query.py:276-280` returns False, `_marked`'s joined-product branch at `src/query.py:586-588` does not refuse, and `Engine.explore` selects the ordinary parent hit at `src/app.py:88-95`. Removing the previous capital/digit fallback exposed this code-normalization mismatch.

Every refusal stage misses the primary: resolver tiers are CONTAINED_ALIAS + UNRESOLVED, `_resolver_blocked={}`, `_qualified=False`, `_item_has_isotope=False`, `_item_joined=False`, every traced `_marked` result is None, `_whole_name=None`, and public linker and both QA results contain no Refused entity. This is independently confirmed through the original CLI server's resolve/explore/ask endpoints, not merely by calling a helper. [Direct stage results](new_results.json), [strict direct/HTTP assertions](assertions.json), [original CLI responses](independent_m27_http.json).

Corroborations: `SN-38 linked to trastuzumab` also leaks (inherited); `trastuzumab linked to DM-1` is another new regression while compact DM1 refuses. `trastuzumab linked to Alexa Fluor 488` and `trastuzumab linked to Alexa Fluor 647` also newly leak. For the Alexa cases, the named-dye vocabulary misses `fluor` (the list contains `fluoro` and `fluoresc`) and contains no Alexa code. These are related recognizer boundaries; adding a reagent noun such as dye is not an acceptable prerequisite for recognizing an already named dye under the stated contract. The helper-only SN-38 normalization experiment does not fix the Alexa boundary.

Identity premise: NCI describes an antibody conjugate using the active metabolite SN-38 as its joined payload. This supports the payload interpretation; it does **not** establish that the audit's trastuzumab-SN-38 description identifies sacituzumab or any unique approved ADC. A product explicitly described as linked to SN-38 must not silently become bare trastuzumab. [NCI Drug Dictionary](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/sacituzumab-govitecan). Thermo Fisher identifies Alexa Fluor 488 as a fluorescent dye attachable to proteins and used in antibody conjugates. [Manufacturer's dye description](https://www.thermofisher.com/jp/ja/home/life-science/cell-analysis/fluorophores/alexa-fluor-488.html).

Causal diagnostic: replacing only the in-memory reagent predicate with an additional token-local canonical-code check causes forward and reverse SN-38 and DM-1 to refuse, retains all tested ordinary-relation controls, and leaves the separate affirmative-focus and Alexa failures intact. Original source, exact replacement text, restoration, and all outputs are saved in [counterfactual.py](counterfactual.py) and [counterfactual.json](counterfactual.json). This experiment isolates the mechanism; it is not a proposed or completeness-tested production patch.

## M27-B2: the only-word exemption leaves other affirmative focus constructions negated (P1, inherited)

Exact input: **`trastuzumab not merely conjugated to DM1 but also purified`**. Both current and previous query modules draw bare **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, with the same full 13-trial map and 12 / 12 QA evidence rows. The statement asserts conjugation and adds purification; it does not deny conjugation. The analogous `not solely` and `not exclusively` complete phrases reproduce it. The repaired **`trastuzumab not only conjugated to DM1 but also purified`** now refuses, as required.

At `src/query.py:263-271`, only the literal next word `only` prevents negation initiation. `merely` still starts a negated span and `.endswith("ly")` carries that span onto `conjugated`, causing the positive product marker to be skipped. There is no later refusal, and the strict all-stage and HTTP equality assertions above pass for each complete focus phrase. Thus the exact M26-B1 input is FIXED, but replacing one semantically affirmative focus word preserves its original product leak.

The helper-only focus-word exclusion makes these three complete affirmatives refuse, leaves the SN-38 and Alexa failures unchanged, and retains the genuine negatives `trastuzumab not conjugated to DM1`, `trastuzumab not covalently linked to DM1`, and `trastuzumab not directly conjugated to DM1`. Combined with the independent code-normalization diagnostic, both primary mechanisms close in the measured controls. The artifact records the literal original condition and diagnostic replacement; no tracked source is edited. [Causal outputs](counterfactual.json).

NCI describes trastuzumab joined to DM1 through an MCC linker as an antibody-drug conjugate. The audit phrase does not specify a linker or unique registered product; the requirement is to avoid drawing the unmodified constituent as the described conjugate. [NCI Drug Dictionary](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/trastuzumab-emtansine).

## Scope, controls and delivery proof

- 22 paired current/previous direct cases; previous means only `query.py` loaded from HEAD^ alongside current other modules and data, not a complete old deployment.
- **8 strict all-stage leak variants**, including **4 new regressions**, each with complete-map and complete-QA-evidence equality assertions; **13 paired controls** pass. `trastuzumab linked to doxorubicin` is retained separately as an unadjudicated two-entity diagnostic and excluded from the strict complete-map equality set.
- **88 original CLI HTTP requests**, all HTTP 200. Port **9947** was closed; the database hash is unchanged; served page bytes match the worktree. [Lifecycle](independent_m27_lifecycle.json).
- The independent DB/UI lane exercised all **22 actual form callbacks**, with **0 render errors**. Leaking cases generate the parent SVG and CHEMBL1201585 trail. This is a Node VM with controlled DOM/fetch, not browser painting. [Page callback outputs](../db_ui/ui_m27_http.json), [callback assertions](../db_ui/callback_assertions.json).
- Latest repair controls: compact SN38/DM1, fluorescein and `not only` conjugation refuse; forward/reverse polyethylene glycol both refuse. All four M26-S1 ordinary clinical-relation examples now retain OSIMERTINIB. No regression among these controls is alleged.
- [Reuse manifest](reuse_manifest.json) records the original M26 scripts, copied hashes and path/port/case adaptations. All new files are under m27/attacks; imports disable bytecode; direct-engine database reads use `mode=ro`; the HTTP lane calls the original CLI. QA reports its local template generator, and no model is called.

Required behavior: recognize the named hyphenated payload under the same identity guard as its compact spelling, and preserve affirmative conjugation semantics beyond the literal `not only` wording while retaining true-negation and ordinary-relation controls. The known accepted over-refusals and owner-curation limitations do not account for either primary finding.
# Independent relation-map review — round 26

Reviewed **HEAD `c42e89530084cd83073ad95090452d89acb4539a`**, on 2026-11-04 (Asia/Seoul). **CHANGES REQUIRED.** Two independently reproduced P1 product-identity mechanisms miss every refusal stage and draw an explicitly joined product as bare TRASTUZUMAB. Both also occur with the previous query module: these are newly demonstrated residual defects, not newly introduced regressions. The exact M25 primary inputs are repaired; that does not establish closure of the two defect classes.

The decisive inputs are **`trastuzumab is not only conjugated to DM1 but also purified`** and **`trastuzumab linked to a derivative of the toxin DM1`**. Both return **CHEMBL1201585**, the complete bare-trastuzumab map with **13 graph trials**, and the same complete **12 trial / 12 sponsor QA evidence rows** as bare trastuzumab. Actual page form callbacks generate its SVG and trail. There is no blocked resolver component or downstream Refused entity for either primary input. [Stage traces](attacks/new_results.json), [original CLI HTTP](checks/independent_m26_http.json), [strict/equality assertions](attacks/http_assertions.json), [actual page callbacks](contract/ui_m26_http.json).

No production patch is proposed or applied. All new scratch and temporary files are under `audit_evidence_claims/m26/`; the existing brief/log are preserved. The user-recorded over-refusals and two owner-curation data limitations are excluded from the blocker decision, and are still reported explicitly in the history matrix.

## Method and limits

Repair notes were treated as hypotheses. Earlier findings are rechecked against their original exact reproduction inputs, with reused scripts, source hashes, path/port adaptations, execution records, and fresh outputs. Primary repairs, surviving supporting cases, accepted over-refusals, data limitations, and cost observations are distinguished below; a corrected primary spelling is not used to erase surviving examples.

The new attack lane ran **39 current/HEAD^ case pairs**. The independent root server ran **156 original CLI HTTP requests**, all HTTP 200, with the worktree page served byte-for-byte. Independent assertions confirm **11 strict all-stage leaking variants** and **15 paired controls**. The **39 actual page callback captures** have no render errors. The owned server on **9935** was closed and the database hash remained unchanged. HTTP 200 is transport success, not identity correctness. [Lifecycle](checks/independent_m26_lifecycle.json), [script reuse](checks/http_reuse_manifest.json), [HTTP assertions](attacks/http_assertions.json), [callback assertions](contract/callback_assertions.json).

HEAD^ comparisons load only the previous `query.py` alongside the current other modules and data, rather than deploying a complete historical checkout. This isolates the latest production-code change. Causal ablations below replace only a helper in memory, restore it afterward, and are diagnostic experiments rather than proposed production repairs. The page harness runs the actual `web/try.html` JavaScript in a Node VM with controlled DOM/fetch objects: it proves generated markup, callback and trail behavior, not browser painting or browser-executed XSS. No model was called.

## M26-B1 — affirmative “not only … but also …” is mistaken for product negation (P1)

**Exact input: `trastuzumab is not only conjugated to DM1 but also purified`.** This affirms conjugation and adds purification. The current map instead displays bare TRASTUZUMAB and returns its complete ordinary QA evidence. The previous query module does the same. The shorter `trastuzumab not only conjugated to DM1` and complete `not only linked` / `not only coupled` variants corroborate the same mechanism. [Direct stages](attacks/new_results.json), [HTTP equality](attacks/http_assertions.json).

**Hypothesis and source:** `_item_joined` at `src/query.py:255-265` begins a negated span after `not` and carries it through any word ending in `ly`. For this input, the outside-name tokens start `is / not / only / conjugated / to / DM1 / but / also / purified`; `only` satisfies `.endswith("ly")`, so **conjugated** is marked negated and skipped. The focus construction is affirmative, but the code treats it like `not covalently conjugated`. No other guard rescues the miss: `_item_has_isotope=False`, `_qualified=False`, `_resolver_blocked={}`, `_marked=None`. The ordinary hit is returned by `link_entities` at `src/query.py:440-449` and selected by `Engine.explore` at `src/app.py:88-95`.

**Causal check:** removing only the `not only` focus construction from joining-negation initiation in memory makes the complete primary description refuse. It leaves the separate article-window examples leaking and preserves the genuinely negated `trastuzumab not covalently linked to DM1` and `trastuzumab not conjugated to DM1` controls. Restoring the helper restores the production result. [Ablation code and exact before/after replacement](attacks/counterfactual.py), [results](attacks/counterfactual.json).

The paired affirmative controls `trastuzumab only conjugated to DM1` and `trastuzumab is conjugated to DM1 and purified` already refuse. Thus the identity failure is introduced by the focus phrase, rather than an inability to recognize DM1 conjugation in general. Required outcome: keep true negation controls accepted while refusing a positively asserted modified product in the complete `not only … but also …` construction.

**Identity premise:** the NCI Drug Dictionary describes ado-trastuzumab emtansine as trastuzumab joined to DM1 through an MCC linker. This supports distinguishing the conjugate from its bare constituent. The audit text does not specify the full linker or a unique registered product, so it need not resolve specifically to emtansine; it must not silently become bare trastuzumab. [NCI primary source](https://www.cancer.gov/publications/dictionaries/cancer-drug/def/trastuzumab-emtansine).

## M26-B2 — article removal occurs after the lookahead is cut (P1)

**Exact input: `trastuzumab linked to a derivative of the toxin DM1`.** Both current and previous query modules return bare TRASTUZUMAB. Deleting only the second article gives `trastuzumab linked to a derivative of toxin DM1`, which the current module correctly refuses. The same minimal distinction holds for `trastuzumab linked to a molecule of the dye IRDye800CW` versus the version without `the`; `linked with a derivative of the toxin DM1` also reproduces the leak. Every refusal stage misses the principal input. [Stage traces](attacks/new_results.json), [HTTP/equality assertions](attacks/http_assertions.json).

**Hypothesis and source:** at `src/query.py:274-279`, the code constructs candidates from `range(i + 2, min(len(raw), i + 6))`, then removes `a/an/the`, then keeps three. It therefore examines at most **four raw words**, not the first three words remaining after articles are skipped. After `to` the primary raw window is **a / derivative / of / the**. Filtering leaves only **derivative / of**, omitting **toxin**, the third nonarticle word, even though `toxin` is explicitly in `_REAGENTS` (`src/query.py:242`). The following code `DM1` is also outside the window. `_item_joined=False` lets `_marked` return None at `src/query.py:586-588,614`, and the same public path draws the originator.

**Causal check:** changing only the raw iteration extent in memory so article filtering precedes the three-word limit makes these article variants refuse. This leaves the `not only` failure unchanged and retains the ordinary controls `osimertinib linked to drug resistance` and `osimertinib linked with poor prognosis`. [Exact diagnostic replacement](attacks/counterfactual.py), [results](attacks/counterfactual.json). This experiment diagnoses the off-by-articles mechanism; it does not establish that a three-word semantic heuristic is generally sufficient.

Required outcome: the same reagent noun within the stated three-nonarticle-word budget must be checked with or without an extra article. No unique ADC identity need be inferred from the generic phrase, but an explicitly toxin-linked substance must not be silently shown as the unmodified antibody.

The counterexample `trastuzumab not only conjugated to DM1 but also PEGylated` is correctly refused by another guard. It is a negative control, not counted among strict all-stage misses. The two causal ablations close their respective mechanisms independently; neither alone closes the other.

## M26-S1 — expanded lookahead introduces ordinary-relation over-refusals (should fix)

A separate seven-case current/HEAD^ probe finds four new refusal regressions: `osimertinib linked to longer PFS`, `osimertinib linked to improved 3-year survival`, `osimertinib linked to response in 2026`, and `osimertinib linked to resistance in EGFR`. The first three now refuse; the last refuses OSIMERTINIB and centres the separately named **EGFR**, whereas HEAD^ centres OSIMERTINIB. The capitalization/digit heuristic at `src/query.py:277-279` now reaches clinical abbreviations and years, although these phrases do not describe joining a payload. [Paired traces](attacks/recall_results.json), [compact comparison](attacks/recall_summary.json).

`osimertinib linked with improved overall survival`, `linked to drug resistance`, and `linked with poor prognosis` remain accepted in both versions. This is a newly measured recall limitation, separate from the user-recorded accepted over-refusals and from fixed-page demo behavior. It is a should-fix issue, not the reason for rejecting the product-identity guarantee.

 succeeded in 237ms:
---
name: review
description: Review the changes since a fixed point (commit, branch, tag, or merge-base) along two axes — Standards (does the code follow this repo's documented coding standards?) and Spec (does the code match what the originating issue/PRD asked for?). Runs both reviews in parallel sub-agents and reports them side by side. Use when the user wants to review a branch, a PR, work-in-progress changes, or asks to "review since X".
---

Two-axis review of the diff between `HEAD` and a fixed point the user supplies:

- **Standards** — does the code conform to this repo's documented coding standards?
- **Spec** — does the code faithfully implement the originating issue / PRD / spec?

Both axes run as **parallel sub-agents** so they don't pollute each other's context, then this skill aggregates their findings.

The issue tracker should have been provided to you — run `/setup-matt-pocock-skills` if `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` is missing.

## Process

### 1. Pin the fixed point

Whatever the user said is the fixed point — a commit SHA, branch name, tag, `main`, `HEAD~5`, etc. If they didn't specify one, ask for it.

Capture the diff command once: `git diff <fixed-point>...HEAD` (three-dot, so the comparison is against the merge-base). Also note the list of commits via `git log <fixed-point>..HEAD --oneline`.

Before going further, confirm the fixed point resolves (`git rev-parse <fixed-point>`) and the diff is non-empty. A bad ref or empty diff should fail here — not inside two parallel sub-agents.

### 2. Identify the spec source

Look for the originating spec, in this order:

1. Issue references in the commit messages (`#123`, `Closes #45`, GitLab `!67`, etc.) — fetch via the workflow in `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.
2. A path the user passed as an argument.
3. A PRD/spec file under `docs/`, `specs/`, or `.scratch/` matching the branch name or feature.
4. If nothing is found, ask the user where the spec is. If they say there isn't one, the **Spec** sub-agent will skip and report "no spec available".

### 3. Identify the standards sources

Anything in the repo that documents how code should be written, such as `CODING_STANDARDS.md` or `CONTRIBUTING.md`.

### 4. Spawn both sub-agents in parallel

Send a single message with two `Agent` tool calls. Use the `general-purpose` subagent for both.

**Standards sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The full diff command and commit list.
- The list of standards-source files you found in step 3.
- The brief: "Report — per file/hunk where relevant — every place the diff violates a documented standard. Cite the standard (file + the rule). Distinguish hard violations from judgement calls. Skip anything tooling enforces. Under 400 words."

**Spec sub-agent prompt** — include:

- The diff command and commit list.
- The path or fetched contents of the spec.
- The brief: "Report: (a) requirements the spec asked for that are missing or partial; (b) behaviour in the diff that wasn't asked for (scope creep); (c) requirements that look implemented but where the implementation looks wrong. Quote the spec line for each finding. Under 400 words."

If the spec is missing, skip the Spec sub-agent and note this in the final report.

### 5. Aggregate

Present the two reports under `## Standards` and `## Spec` headings, verbatim or lightly cleaned. Do **not** merge or rerank findings — the two axes are deliberately separate (see _Why two axes_).

End with a one-line summary: total findings per axis, and the worst issue _within each axis_ (if any). Don't pick a single winner across axes — that's the reranking the separation exists to prevent.

## Why two axes

A change can pass one axis and fail the other:

- Code that follows every standard but implements the wrong thing → **Standards pass, Spec fail.**
- Code that does exactly what the issue asked but breaks the project's conventions → **Spec pass, Standards fail.**

Reporting them separately stops one axis from masking the other.

"""Grounded question answering over the graph.

The retrieval step is the same resolver the pipeline uses, pointed at the
question instead of at a registry record. A question that names "Tagrisso" and a
graph that stores CHEMBL3353410 only meet if one vocabulary serves both, so the
dictionary is reused rather than reimplemented.

Three properties the answering layer holds to:

* **Every statement is traceable.** Each claim carries the edges it rests on,
  each edge carries its source record and fetch time.
* **The graph answers, not the model.** With a key, the language model is given
  the retrieved evidence and returns claims, not prose; each claim is checked
  against the graph and the answer is written from the accepted ones in the
  graph's own words (structured.py). With no key the evidence is rendered
  directly.
* **Nothing unsupported is shipped.** A claim the graph does not support is
  reported and left out. An unverifiable answer is worse than a short one.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import os
import functools
import re
import sqlite3
import sys
import unicodedata
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T

DB_PATH = C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite"
TRIAL_URL = "https://clinicaltrials.gov/study/{}"


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# evidence
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
@dataclass
class Evidence:
    statement: str
    edge_id: str
    relation: str
    source: str
    source_record: str | None
    fetched_at: str | None
    confidence: float
    tier: str | None = None
    url: str | None = None
    detail: dict = field(default_factory=dict)

    def citation(self) -> str:
        base = self.source_record or self.source
        return f"[{base}]"


@dataclass
class Answer:
    question: str
    intent: str
    entities: list[dict]
    text: str
    evidence: list[Evidence]
    generated_by: str
    checks: dict

    def to_dict(self) -> dict:
        return {
            "question": self.question,
            "intent": self.intent,
            "entities": self.entities,
            "answer": self.text,
            "generated_by": self.generated_by,
            "checks": self.checks,
            "evidence": [
                {"statement": e.statement, "relation": e.relation, "edge_id": e.edge_id,
                 "source": e.source, "source_record": e.source_record,
                 "fetched_at": e.fetched_at, "confidence": e.confidence,
                 "resolution_tier": e.tier, "url": e.url, **e.detail}
                for e in self.evidence
            ],
        }


# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# engine
# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #
# Words that list separately named things; a list item ends at one. "with" is not among
# them, because it also sits inside product descriptions ("paclitaxel with albumin bound
# nanoparticles").
_JOINERS = {"and", "or", "plus", "versus", "vs", "vs.", "+", "&", "then", "followed",
            "or/and", "and/or"}



# every Unicode dash (category Pd) and minus sign, read as a hyphen
_DASHES = "[" + "".join(re.escape(chr(c)) for c in range(0x110000)
                        if unicodedata.category(chr(c)) == "Pd" or chr(c) in "\u2212\u207b\u208b") + "]"


def _plain(text: str) -> str:
    """Accents off, as the alias key does."""
    t = unicodedata.normalize("NFKD", text or "")
    return unicodedata.normalize("NFC", "".join(c for c in t if not unicodedata.combining(c)))


def _alnum_words(text: str) -> list[str]:
    """Letters-and-digits words, with every other character a separator."""
    return re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(text))


def _fold(text: str) -> str:
    return re.sub(_DASHES, "-", unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text or ""))



# Mass numbers of the isotopes used in medicine, per element (lower case).
# Radioactive labels only: stable 2H, 13C and 15N mark chemistry ("13-cis"), not tracers.
_MEDICAL_MASS = {"h": {3}, "c": {11, 14}, "n": {13}, "o": {15}, "f": {18},
                 "p": {32}, "s": {35}, "cu": {61, 62, 64, 67}, "ga": {67, 68}, "br": {76, 77},
                 "rb": {82}, "sr": {89, 90}, "y": {86, 90}, "zr": {89}, "tc": {99}, "in": {111},
                 "i": {123, 124, 125, 131}, "sm": {153}, "lu": {177}, "re": {186, 188},
                 "pb": {203, 212}, "at": {211}, "ra": {223}, "ac": {225},
                 # therapy and imaging nuclides whose symbols do not start common words
                 "tb": {149, 152, 155, 161}, "th": {227}, "bi": {212, 213}, "sc": {43, 44, 47},
                 "ho": {166}, "tl": {201}, "xe": {133}, "au": {198}, "pd": {103},
                 "ir": {192}, "ag": {111}, "sn": {117}, "dy": {165}, "pt": {195}}
# Further nuclides, recognised only beside their written-out element name: their symbols
# begin ordinary words ("75 several", "51 criteria"), so a bare symbol would over-refuse.
_NAME_MASS = {**_MEDICAL_MASS, "er": {169}, "se": {75}, "cr": {51}, "co": {57, 58, 60},
              "ce": {134}, "la": {132, 135}, "fe": {59}, "cs": {131, 137}, "mn": {52}}


def _medical_isotope(label: str) -> bool:
    m = re.match(r"^(\d{1,3})m?([a-z]{1,2})$", (label or "").lower())
    return bool(m and int(m.group(1)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(2), set()))


def _medical_isotope_in(key: str, one_letter: bool = True) -> bool:
    """Is a medical isotope written anywhere in this run-together key, mass first ("89zr",
    "a33124i") or a two-letter element first ("lu177")? One-letter elements only when
    `one_letter` (within a word: "3hvinblastine"; across words "13 nsclc" is not N-13)."""
    for m in re.finditer(r"(\d+)m?([a-z]{1,2})", key):
        mass = int(m.group(1)[-3:])
        el = m.group(2)
        if mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(el, set()):
            return True
        if one_letter and mass in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(el[:1], set()):
            return True
    for m in re.finditer(r"([a-z]{2})(\d{2,3})(?!\d)", key):
        if int(m.group(2)) in _MEDICAL_MASS.get(m.group(1), set()):
            return True
    return False




# Element names written out, mapped to the symbols the mass table uses.
_ELEMENT_NAMES = {"hydrogen": "h", "tritium": "h", "carbon": "c", "nitrogen": "n", "oxygen": "o",
                  "fluorine": "f", "phosphorus": "p", "sulfur": "s", "sulphur": "s",
                  "copper": "cu", "gallium": "ga", "bromine": "br", "rubidium": "rb",
                  "strontium": "sr", "yttrium": "y", "zirconium": "zr", "technetium": "tc",
                  "indium": "in", "iodine": "i", "samarium": "sm", "lutetium": "lu",
                  "lutecium": "lu", "rhenium": "re", "lead": "pb", "astatine": "at",
                  "radium": "ra", "actinium": "ac", "terbium": "tb", "thorium": "th",
                  "bismuth": "bi", "scandium": "sc", "holmium": "ho", "thallium": "tl",
                  "xenon": "xe", "gold": "au", "palladium": "pd", "iridium": "ir",
                  "silver": "ag", "tin": "sn", "dysprosium": "dy", "platinum": "pt",
                  "erbium": "er", "selenium": "se", "chromium": "cr", "cobalt": "co",
                  "cerium": "ce", "lanthanum": "la", "iron": "fe", "caesium": "cs",
                  "cesium": "cs", "manganese": "mn"}
# Stems that say the substance carries a radioactive label, read on a word or on two or
# three neighbouring words glued ("radio-labelled", "radio-immuno-conjugate").
_LABEL_STEMS = ("radiolabel", "radioiod", "radioactiv", "radioconjug", "radioimmuno",
                "radiotracer", "radioligand", "radionuclid", "radiopharm", "tritiat",
                "radioisotop", "radiometal", "radiochelat", "radiohalogen", "radiotheranost",
                "astatinat")
# Any other "radio..." word is read as a label (fail closed), except these, which name a
# treatment or a method, not a labelled substance ("radiotherapy plus cisplatin")
_RADIO_SAFE = ("radiotherap", "radiation", "radiolog", "radiograph", "radiosurg", "radiosensiti",
               "radiofrequen", "radiomic", "radioresist", "radiochemotherap", "radioprotect",
               "radiodermat", "radiotoxic", "radioresponsive", "radiocurab", "radiolucen",
               "radiopaque", "radiopacit", "radiolysis", "radiologic", "radiograph")
# "labelled" on its own: the substance carries a label of some kind ("isotope-labelled",
# "FITC-labelled"); "unlabelled" and "non-labelled" do not count
_LABELLED = {"labelled", "labeled", "labelling", "labeling", "tagged"}
# A word just before the label that says the opposite ("non-radio-labelled")
_NOT_WORDS = {"non", "not", "no", "un", "without"}
# Symbols that are also everyday abbreviations: read beside a mass only inside one word
_SPACED_NOT = {"cr", "co", "se", "la"}
# Words that say the substance carries a radioactive label.
_LABEL_WORDS = {"tritiated", "tritium", "radiotracer", "radiotracers", "radioligand", "radionuclide",
                "radioconjugate", "radioimmunoconjugate", "radiopharmaceutical", "radioactive",
                "radiolabelled", "radiolabeled", "radiolabel"}

def _strong_label(x: str) -> bool:
    return x in _LABEL_WORDS or x in _LABELLED or x.startswith(_LABEL_STEMS)


def _negated_end(flat: list, i: int) -> int:
    """The end of the label a negation just before flat[i] governs: the shortest glued run
    that is a known label ("non-labelled", "non-radio-labelled"), a bare "radio" with the
    word it prefixes ("non-radio-astatinated"), and a label after its adverb ("not
    radioactively labelled"); never a separate later label ("non-labelled radiolabelled")."""
    end = i + 1
    for j in range(i + 1, min(len(flat), i + 3) + 1):
        if _strong_label("".join(flat[i:j])):
            end = j
            break
    else:
        if flat[i] == "radio" and i + 1 < len(flat):
            end = i + 2
    if flat[end - 1].endswith("ly") and end < len(flat) and _strong_label(flat[end]):
        end += 1
    return end


# Words that say the drug is joined to something else (a dye, a payload): a distinct
# product ("trastuzumab conjugated to DM1", "IRDye800CW-conjugated trastuzumab")
_JOINED = ("conjugat", "covalently", "bioconjugat", "immunoconjugat", "coupled", "coupling",
           "attached", "tethered", "crosslinked", "chemically")
# Payload words that mark a drug's own name as a conjugate (TRASTUZUMAB EMTANSINE,
# ETIRINOTECAN PEGOL): a later word of a several-word name, never the molecule itself
# (IRINOTECAN, TOPOTECAN, TASIDOTIN carry the same letters but are plain molecules)
_PAYLOAD = re.compile(r"\w*(tansine|tecan|dotin|ozogamicin|tesirine|duocarmazine|pegol|"
                      r"sudotox|aritox|pasudotox)|conjugate", re.I)


def _payload_name(name: str) -> bool:
    words = re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", name or "")
    return any(_PAYLOAD.fullmatch(w) for w in words[1:]) or "conjugate" in name.lower()


# Payload, dye, carrier and chelator codes ("DM1", "MMAE", "SN-38", "PEG", "DOTA", "FITC")
_PAYLOAD_CODES = {"dm1", "dm4", "mmae", "mmaf", "sn38", "dxd", "pbd", "peg", "dota", "nota",
                  "dfo", "dtpa", "fitc", "cy5", "cy7", "cy55", "mcc", "smcc", "vcmmae", "calicheamicin",
                  "emtansine", "mertansine", "deruxtecan", "vedotin", "biotin", "hrp", "gfp"}
# "not only", "not merely": a focus, not a negation ("not only conjugated ... but also purified")
_FOCUS = {"only", "merely", "solely", "exclusively", "just", "simply", "purely"}
# Prefix pieces a negation carries through ("non-radio-immuno-conjugated")
_PIECES = {"radio", "immuno", "bio", "chemo", "photo", "nano"}
# Nouns that name what a drug is joined to ("linked to polyethylene glycol")
_REAGENTS = ("polyethylene", "glycol", "peg", "payload", "toxin", "dye", "fluoro", "fluoresc",
             "chelat", "polymer", "rhodamin", "cyanin", "nanoparticle", "liposom", "albumin", "biotin", "maytansin",
             "auristatin", "peptide", "linker", "cytotox",
             "radionuclid", "isotop", "dextran", "dendrimer", "micelle", "lipid")


@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=4096)
def _item_joined(words: tuple) -> bool:
    """Does this list item say the drug is conjugated or covalently linked to something?"""
    raw = _alnum_words(" ".join(words))
    flat = [x.lower() for x in raw]
    negated: set = set()
    # what the drug would be joined to, anywhere in the item and in either order
    # ("linked to a derivative of the toxin DM1", "polyethylene glycol linked to irinotecan")
    # each word, and each neighbouring pair glued, so a hyphenated code counts ("SN-38", "DM-1")
    pieces = flat + [a + b for a, b in zip(flat, flat[1:])]
    reagent = any(x.startswith(_REAGENTS) or x in _PAYLOAD_CODES or x.startswith(("irdye", "alexa"))
                  for x in pieces)
    for i, x in enumerate(flat):
        if i and flat[i - 1] in _NOT_WORDS and x not in _FOCUS:
            # "not only / merely / solely ... but also" affirms; otherwise the negation covers the one word it negates, carried on through an adverb
            # or a prefix piece ("not covalently linked", "non-radio-immuno-conjugated"),
            # never across to a separate affirmative ("not radiolabelled but conjugated")
            j = i
            negated.add(j)
            while j + 1 < len(flat) and (flat[j].endswith("ly") or flat[j] in _PIECES):
                j += 1
                negated.add(j)
        if i in negated:
            continue
        if x.startswith(_JOINED):
            return True
        if x == "linked":
            # "linked via a linker to", or "linked" with a payload, dye or carrier named in
            # the item; not a relation in words ("osimertinib linked to longer PFS")
            if "via" in flat[i + 1:i + 4] or reagent:
                return True
    return False


@functools.lru_cache(maxsize=4096)
def _item_has_isotope(words: tuple) -> bool:
    """Is a medical isotope written anywhere in this list item? Worked out once per item.

    Two-letter elements are read across words ("trastuzumab Ga 68"); a one-letter element
    only inside one word, mass first ("3hvinblastine") or element first ("I-124-labelled"),
    so "13 NSCLC" is not N-13 and "BKM120" is not M-120."""
    if _medical_isotope_in(T.canonical_key(" ".join(words)), one_letter=False):
        return True
    flat = [x.lower() for x in _alnum_words(" ".join(words))]
    # each word, and two or three neighbouring words glued ("radio-labelled",
    # "radio-immuno-conjugate"), unless a negation stands just before ("non-radio-labelled")
    negated: set = set()                     # the up-to-three words a negation governs
    for i in range(len(flat)):
        if i and flat[i - 1] in _NOT_WORDS:
            negated.update(range(i, _negated_end(flat, i)))
        if i in negated:
            continue
        for j in range(i + 1, min(len(flat), i + 3) + 1):
            x = "".join(flat[i:j])
            if x in _LABEL_WORDS or x.startswith(_LABEL_STEMS):
                return True                  # "radiolabelled", "tritiated", "radiotracer"
        if flat[i] in _LABELLED:
            return True
        if flat[i].startswith("radio"):
            runs = ["".join(flat[i:j]) for j in range(i + 1, min(len(flat), i + 3) + 1)]
            if not any(r.startswith(_RADIO_SAFE) for r in runs):
                return True                  # "radioastatinated", "radio-fluorinated"

    def named(name: str, num: str, apart: bool = False) -> bool:
        el = _ELEMENT_NAMES.get(name)
        if el is None and len(name) == 2 and name in _NAME_MASS:
            # a two-letter symbol counts as written ("Ce-134", "[134Ce]"); across a space
            # only when it is not also a common abbreviation ("CR 51" = complete response)
            if apart and name in _SPACED_NOT:
                return False
            el = name
        m = re.fullmatch(r"(\d{1,3})m?", num)
        return bool(el and m and int(m.group(1)) in _NAME_MASS.get(el, set()))

    # element names or symbols beside a mass, in either order, apart or glued
    # ("iodine-124", "99m-technetium", "iodine124", "technetium99m", "Ce 134")
    for a, b in zip(flat, flat[1:]):
"""A local page for trying the pipeline by hand.

    python src/app.py            then open http://localhost:8765

A relation map (a node and the nodes live edges tie it to, a capped list per relation,
click to move on),
then four boxes: type an intervention string the way a registry would write it and see
how the resolver reads it, or ask a question and see the grounded answer with its
evidence, give a structured answer (claims citing evidence edges) and see which
claims the graph supports and the answer written from them, or paste free prose and
see what the auxiliary word-order check flags. All of them call
the functions the pipeline uses (resolve_drug_string, OncoKG.answer,
OncoKG.check_groundedness) on the built artefacts, so what the page shows is what the
pipeline would do. Nothing is written; the language model is never called.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import json
import sqlite3
import sys
import threading
from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
from pathlib import Path

sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent))
import config as C
import normalize as N
import textrules as T
from query import OncoKG

PAGE = C.WEB / "try.html"
PORT = 8765


class Engine:
    def __init__(self):
        self.kg = OncoKG()
        # A browser opens spare connections it may never use, which would stall a
        # single-threaded server; requests are threaded, the engine is not.
        self.kg.conn.close()
        self.kg.conn = sqlite3.connect(C.PROCESSED / "oncokg.sqlite", check_same_thread=False)
        self.kg.conn.row_factory = sqlite3.Row
        self.lock = threading.Lock()
        self.dic = self.kg.dic
        self.index_keys = self.kg.index_keys
        target_rows, _, _ = N._load("targets")
        self.target_keys = N.target_guard_keys(target_rows, self.dic)

    def resolve(self, text: str) -> dict:
        out = N.resolve_drug_string(text, self.dic, self.index_keys, self.target_keys)
        for c in out["components"]:
            d = self.dic["drugs"].get(c.get("drug_id") or "")
            if d:
                c["drug"] = {k: d.get(k) for k in
                             ("name", "drug_type", "max_phase_overall", "max_phase_in_nsclc")}
                c["drug"]["mechanisms"] = [m.get("mechanismOfAction") if isinstance(m, dict) else m
                                           for m in (d.get("mechanisms") or [])][:3]
                c["drug"]["trials"] = self.kg.conn.execute(
                    "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                    (d["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]
                par = self.dic["drugs"].get(d.get("parent_molecule") or "")
                if par:
                    c["drug"]["parent"] = {"name": par["name"], "trials": self.kg.conn.execute(
                        "SELECT COUNT(*) FROM edge WHERE rel='EVALUATES' AND dst=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                        (par["drug_id"],)).fetchone()[0]}
            if c.get("candidates"):
                c["candidate_names"] = [
                    (self.dic["drugs"].get(x) or {}).get("name", x) for x in c["candidates"]]
        return out

    def explore(self, text: str, per_group: int = 30) -> dict:
        """One live node and the live nodes its live edges tie it to, grouped by relation.

        `text` is a node id or a name the question linker reads as one entity. A name
        the linker refuses (a biosimilar, a reformulation, a tracer) is reported, never
        mapped to the originator. Each group keeps `per_group` neighbours, sorted as the
        page states: trials by start date (latest first), drugs by trials in the graph,
        sponsors by trials they lead, disease targets by association score. Sponsorship
        keeps its role (lead sponsor or collaborator)."""
        conn = self.kg.conn
        also: list = []
        matched = None
        row = conn.execute("SELECT id FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                           (text,)).fetchone()
        if row:
            nid = row["id"]
        else:
            ents = self.kg.link_entities(text)
            refused = [e for e in ents if e["type"] == "Refused"]
            hits = [e for e in ents if e.get("id") and e["type"] != "Refused"]
            if not hits:
                if refused:
                    return {"refused": refused[0]["label"], "why": refused[0]["why"]}
                return {"not_found": text}
            nid, matched = hits[0]["id"], hits[0].get("matched_alias") or hits[0].get("matched_text")
            also = [{"id": e["id"], "type": e["type"], "label": e["label"]} for e in hits[1:]]
            for oid in hits[0].get("also_matching_ids") or []:   # one alias, several ids
                lab = conn.execute("SELECT type, label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                   (oid,)).fetchone()
                if lab and oid != nid:
                    also.append({"id": oid, "type": lab["type"], "label": lab["label"]})
            # the same words may also name an organisation or a target the linker
            # passed over because a drug alias came first ("Roche" is a folic acid alias)
            key = T.canonical_key(text)
            for cid in self.kg.companies.get(key, []):
                if cid != nid and all(x.get("id") != cid for x in also):
                    lab = conn.execute("SELECT label FROM node WHERE id=? AND retired_at IS NULL",
                                       (cid,)).fetchone()
                    if lab:
                        also.append({"id": cid, "type": "Company", "label": lab["label"]})
            t = self.kg.targets.get(text.strip().lower())
            if t and t["target_id"] != nid:
                also.append({"id": t["target_id"], "type": "Target", "label": t["symbol"]})
            also += [{"refused": e["label"]} for e in refused]
        node = conn.execute("SELECT id, type, label, props FROM node WHERE id=? "
                            "AND retired_at IS NULL", (nid,)).fetchone()
        if node is None:                              # a name can outlive its node
            return {"not_found": text}
        rows = conn.execute(
            "SELECT e.id AS eid, e.rel, e.src, e.props AS eprops, n.id, n.type, n.label, "
            "n.props AS nprops FROM edge e JOIN node n "
            "ON n.id = CASE WHEN e.src=? THEN e.dst ELSE e.src END "
            "WHERE (e.src=? OR e.dst=?) AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND n.retired_at IS NULL",
            (nid, nid, nid)).fetchall()
        drug_ids = list({r["id"] for r in rows if r["type"] == "Drug"} | {nid})
        trials = {}
        for i in range(0, len(drug_ids), 500):
            chunk = drug_ids[i:i + 500]
            trials.update(conn.execute(               # live trials only
                "SELECT e.dst, COUNT(*) FROM edge e JOIN node t ON t.id = e.src "
                "WHERE e.rel='EVALUATES' AND e.retired_at IS NULL AND t.retired_at IS NULL "
                f"AND e.dst IN ({','.join('?' * len(chunk))}) GROUP BY e.dst", chunk).fetchall())
        groups: dict = {}
        for r in rows:
            ep, np_ = _obj(r["eprops"]), _obj(r["nprops"])
            item = {"id": r["id"], "type": r["type"], "label": r["label"], "edge_id": r["eid"]}
            role = ep.get("role") if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS" else None
            if r["type"] == "Drug":
                item["trials"] = trials.get(r["id"], 0)
                item["max_phase"] = np_.get("max_phase_overall")
            if r["rel"] == "SPONSORS_TRIAL_OF":
                n = ep.get("evidence_trials")
                # a count the edge does not record stays unknown; 0 is only for sorting
                item["evidence_trials"] = n if _number(n) else None
                item["weight"] = n if _number(n) else 0
                item["sponsor_class"] = ep.get("sponsor_class")
            elif r["type"] == "Trial":
                item.update({k: np_.get(k) for k in ("phases", "status", "title", "start_date", "url")})
                item["weight"] = str(np_.get("start_date") or "")
            elif r["type"] == "Drug":

"""Independent current CLI HTTP confirmation of negation and linked-phrase attacks and repair controls."""
import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import socket
import subprocess
import sys
import time
import urllib.error
import urllib.request

OUT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT = OUT.parents[2]
PORT = 9947
BASE = f"http://127.0.0.1:{PORT}"
CASES = ['trastuzumab', 'trastuzumab linked to SN-38', 'SN-38 linked to trastuzumab', 'trastuzumab linked to SN38', 'trastuzumab linked to DM-1', 'trastuzumab linked to DM1', 'trastuzumab linked to Alexa Fluor 488', 'trastuzumab linked to Alexa Fluor 647', 'trastuzumab linked to fluorescein', 'trastuzumab linked to doxorubicin', 'trastuzumab not merely conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not solely conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not exclusively conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not only conjugated to DM1 but also purified', 'trastuzumab not directly conjugated to DM1', 'trastuzumab is not only linked to DM1 but also purified', 'irinotecan linked to polyethylene glycol', 'polyethylene glycol linked to irinotecan', 'osimertinib linked to longer PFS', 'osimertinib linked to improved 3-year survival', 'osimertinib linked to response in 2026', 'osimertinib linked to resistance in EGFR']


def post(endpoint, text):
    req = urllib.request.Request(BASE + "/api/" + endpoint,
        data=json.dumps({"text": text}).encode(),
        headers={"Content-Type": "application/json"})
    start = time.perf_counter()
    try:
        response = urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=30)
    except urllib.error.HTTPError as error:
        response = error
    with response:
        return {"endpoint": endpoint, "input": text, "status": response.status,
                "seconds": time.perf_counter() - start,
                "response": json.loads(response.read())}

(OUT/"tmp").mkdir(exist_ok=True)
with socket.socket() as sock:
    assert sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0, "owned port already occupied"
db = ROOT / "data/processed/oncokg.sqlite"
before = hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
env = {**os.environ, "PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE": "1", "PYTHONUTF8": "1", "PYTHONIOENCODING": "utf-8", "TMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TEMP":str(OUT/"tmp"), "TMPDIR":str(OUT/"tmp")}
with (OUT / "independent_m27_server.txt").open("w", encoding="utf-8") as log:
    proc = subprocess.Popen([sys.executable, "-B", "-u", "src/app.py", str(PORT)],
        cwd=ROOT, env=env, stdout=log, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT,
        creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0))
    results = []
    try:
        for _ in range(100):
            assert proc.poll() is None, "server exited"
            try:
                page = urllib.request.urlopen(BASE, timeout=1).read()
                break
            except OSError:
                time.sleep(.1)
        else:
            raise RuntimeError("server unavailable")
        for name in CASES:
            for endpoint in ("resolve", "explore"):
                results.append(post(endpoint, name))
            if not name.startswith("CHEMBL"):
                results.append(post("ask", f"Which trials evaluate {name}?"))
                results.append(post("ask", f"Who sponsors trials of {name}?"))
        (OUT / "independent_m27_http.json").write_text(json.dumps(results,
            ensure_ascii=False, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
        print(json.dumps([{"input": r["input"], "status": r["status"],
            "center": r["response"].get("center", {}).get("id"),
            "refused": r["response"].get("refused")}
            for r in results if r["endpoint"] == "explore"], indent=2))
    finally:
        proc.terminate()
        proc.wait(timeout=10)
        with socket.socket() as sock:
            closed = sock.connect_ex(("127.0.0.1", PORT)) != 0
        (OUT / "independent_m27_lifecycle.json").write_text(json.dumps({
            "pid": proc.pid, "port": PORT, "port_closed": closed,
            "database_unchanged": hashlib.sha256(db.read_bytes()).hexdigest() == before,
            "requests": len(results), "all_200": all(r["status"] == 200 for r in results),
            "page_matches_worktree": page == (ROOT / "web/try.html").read_bytes()
        }, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")

"""Cross-check IDs cited by prior reports against the assembled current report.

This checks report coverage only; execution manifests, not this string check,
establish that original reproduction scripts were run.
"""
import json, re
from pathlib import Path
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
rows=[]
for round_no in range(1,26):
    p=ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims'/f'm{round_no}'/'REPORT.md'
    if round_no==2 and not p.exists():
        p=ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m2b/REPORT.md'
    text=p.read_text(encoding='utf8')
    ids=sorted(set(re.findall(r'\bM\d+[-–][BSD]\d+\b',text)))
    rows.append({'round':round_no,'report':p.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),'referenced_finding_ids':ids})
report=OUT.parent/'REPORT.md'
result={'note':__doc__,'prior_reports':rows}
if report.exists():
    current=report.read_text(encoding='utf8')
    ids=sorted({s for row in rows for s in row['referenced_finding_ids']})
    result['unique_prior_finding_ids']=ids
    result['missing_finding_ids']=[s for s in ids if s not in current]
(OUT/'report_coverage.json').write_text(json.dumps(result,indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps({'prior_reports':len(rows),'unique_finding_ids':len({s for row in rows for s in row['referenced_finding_ids']}),'missing':result.get('missing_finding_ids','report not yet assembled')}))

from pathlib import Path
import ast,hashlib,json
OUT=Path(__file__).resolve().parent
ROOT=OUT.parents[2]
src=ROOT/'audit_evidence_claims/m27/attacks/independent_m27_http.py'
code=src.read_text(encoding='utf8').replace('m27','m28').replace('PORT = 9947','PORT = 9955')
cases=json.loads((OUT.parent/'attacks/cases.json').read_text(encoding='utf8'))
for case in ['b.i.o.similar bevacizumab','lip.osomal doxorubicin']:
 if case not in cases: cases.append(case)
tree=ast.parse(code)
node=next(n for n in tree.body if isinstance(n,ast.Assign) and any(isinstance(t,ast.Name) and t.id=='CASES' for t in n.targets))
lines=code.splitlines(keepends=True)
code=''.join(lines[:node.lineno-1])+'CASES = '+repr(cases)+'\\n'+''.join(lines[node.end_lineno:])
ast.parse(code)
dest=OUT/'independent_m28_http.py'
dest.write_text(code,encoding='utf8')
(OUT/'http_reuse_manifest.json').write_text(json.dumps({'source':src.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),'source_sha256':hashlib.sha256(src.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'destination':dest.relative_to(ROOT).as_posix(),'adapted_sha256':hashlib.sha256(dest.read_bytes()).hexdigest(),'cases':cases,'adaptations':'output round m28, port 9955, attack cases plus two historical residuals'},indent=2),encoding='utf8')
print(json.dumps({'cases':len(cases),'port':9955}))

### M24 joining-guard rescan cost: NOT FIXED as residual overhead

The guard at `src/query.py:586-587` constructs a cache key from the entire list item minus each matched span. With **`' '.join(f'isa x{i:04d}' for i in range(400))`** (3,999 characters, one list item), each removal yields a different tuple. The separate direct instrumented pass records **400 misses / 319,600 words / 1,278,800 aggregate characters** processed by `_item_joined`; its wrapped guard time is 0.26195 s out of 1.01743 s total. At 50 matches / 499 characters it records 50 misses / 4,950 words. These counts demonstrate growing rescan work, not merely host timing variation. [Instrumentation](cost/joined_instrumentation.json).

For the same 3,999-character workload, the ABBA workers retain the same accepted entity in HEAD and HEAD^. HEAD / HEAD^ medians-of-worker-medians are **0.57650 / 0.54438 s**, and first-call cold medians are **0.87195 / 0.75794 s**. The original CLI HTTP check completes in **1.50879 s**, queues an EGFR map for **1.42386 s**, and a warm repeat completes in **1.61150 s**, all 200. [ABBA samples](cost/joined_cost.json), [HTTP](cost/isolated_joined_unique_cost_http.json). This is a **should-fix cost observation**, with no timeout or incorrect map proven by the performance tests. The same ABBA suite covers 499-character matched inputs, repeated conjugation descriptions, a conjugation after many aliases, and negated-adverb text; each completes under the existing character caps. Changing acceptance to refusal is recorded where expected and is not presented as a same-semantics speed comparison.

## Independent specification axis

Reviewed `git diff HEAD~1...HEAD -- src/query.py tests/test_app.py` at `c42e895`. The user requires that a distinct product is refused rather than drawn as its originator. No scope creep was found. [Independent 20-case stage probe](history/spec_probe.json), [summary](history/spec_summary.json).

The exact M25 separated-affirmative negation and article-bearing DM1 examples now refuse, as does `irinotecan linked to polyethylene glycol`. Genuine negation (`not covalently linked`, `not chemically attached`) and ordinary relation controls (`linked to drug resistance`, `linked with poor prognosis`) retain their intended molecules. Known conjugates retain their distinct identities.

Three gaps remain relative to the original identity guarantee:

1. **Focus adverbs are treated as negation of the joining word.** `trastuzumab not only conjugated to DM1` draws bare TRASTUZUMAB. At `src/query.py:255-265`, the `endswith("ly")` condition carries `not` through `only` to suppress affirmative `conjugated`. The root's attack lane corroborates the complete construction `trastuzumab is not only conjugated to DM1 but also purified`. Neither is an actual denial of conjugation.
2. **The three-word target window still misses explicit payloads.** `trastuzumab linked to the small highly potent DM1 payload` draws TRASTUZUMAB because `src/query.py:275-279` checks only `small / highly / potent`. This implements the requested bounded heuristic, but does not satisfy the broader distinct-product guarantee. The paired shorter `...linked to a small cytotoxic payload` refuses.
3. **Reversed attachment remains a historical leak.** `polyethylene glycol linked to irinotecan` draws IRINOTECAN. At `src/query.py:586-588`, removing the matched drug span leaves `polyethylene glycol linked to`; `src/query.py:274-279` tests only the empty post-preposition object. Thus this exact M24 corroboration remains unfixed despite the forward repair.

Each principal has resolver tiers CONTAINED_ALIAS/UNRESOLVED, empty `_resolver_blocked`, false qualified/isotope checks, `_marked=None`, and no public Refused entity. TRASTUZUMAB receives 13 graph trials and 12/12 trial/sponsor QA rows; IRINOTECAN receives 5 graph trials and 5/4 rows. All also occur with HEAD^; these are surviving gaps, not newly introduced regressions. Historical comparisons substitute only query.py with current other modules/data. The root's HTTP and chemistry-source checks establish the end-to-end identity premises for its promoted findings.

## Approval decision

**M26-B1 and M26-B2 violate the product-identity guarantee.** Each principal input affirmatively describes a modified product, every refusal stage misses it, and the map and QA evidence equal the complete bare-originator response. These are newly demonstrated inherited leaks: both also occur under the previous query module. The exact M25 primary reproductions are fixed, but those repairs and the 353 passing tests do not close the independently reproduced mechanisms.

The exact reverse-PEG supporting input **`polyethylene glycol linked to irinotecan`** also remains **NOT FIXED** from M24-B1: it resolves to IRINOTECAN (CHEMBL481), five graph trials and five trial / four sponsor QA evidence rows, with no refusal stage blocking it. The forward input is fixed. This surviving old support is retained in the historical disposition rather than silently counted fixed or promoted as a third new finding. [Independent stage probe](history/spec_probe.json).

No new live-edge membership, direction/relation wording, stored-value, fixed-page demo or exercised generated-markup injection failure was reproduced. Four new ordinary-relation over-refusals, inherited latency costs and documentation qualifications are should-fix items. User-accepted over-refusals and the ChEMBL bevacizumab-code / AACT isotretinoin–TRETINOIN data limitations remain separately recorded, pending the owner's curation decision. Other historical NOT FIXED rows—including fragmented qualifiers, reversed formulations, prefixed comma-name QA and unadjudicated isotope/product diagnostics—remain explicitly unresolved; they are not silently waived or added to the user's accepted exceptions.

Standards axis: zero documented-standard violations. Specification axis: three reported coverage gaps (focus negation, further-distance payload wording, reversed attachment), independently corroborating the current identity boundary. The root's two fully adjudicated mechanisms alone are sufficient for CHANGES REQUIRED.

        # a label fused to a neighbouring word, such as a chelator ("DFO89Zr", "89ZrDFO")
        for w in nxt_all[:2] + prev_all[-2:]:
            if _isotope_edge(w):
                return f"{name} ({w})"
        for ws in inside + [prev[-1:], prev, nxt[:1], nxt]:
            if ws and T.is_isotope_label(" ".join(ws)):
                return name if ws in inside else f"{name} ({' '.join(ws)})"
        # an isotope glued to the name itself ("89zr-trastuzumab" read through "[89Z.r]")
        # anything may sit between them (a chelator: "89ZrDFO-daratumumab"), so the rest
        # needs only to end, or start, with a name of the same drug
        # A medical isotope anywhere in the list item once its letters and digits are run
        # together, however it is split or cased ("89-ZrDFO trastuzumab", "3hvinblastine",
        # "trastuzumab Ga68"), refuses an unlabelled drug. A drug whose own name carries a
        # label (LUTETIUM LU 177 DOTATATE, FLUDEOXYGLUCOSE F 18) is the labelled product.
        if (_item_has_isotope(tuple(tokens[k] for k in same))
                and not _labelled(self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"])):
            return name
        # a joining word inside the matched name is the conjugate's own name
        # ("cbr96 - doxorubicin immunoconjugate"), so only the rest of the item counts
        if (_item_joined(tuple(tokens[k] for k in same if not a <= k < b))
                and not _payload_name(self.dic["drugs"][did]["name"])):
            return name
        # A one-letter element is left to the case-marked word check above: lower-case
        # chemistry ("13-cis retinoic acid") is not carbon-13.
        # The drug's name may sit anywhere in the rest ("89Zrpanitumumab-PET-CT"). A
        # one-letter element counts where it is written upper-case in the name
        # ("3HVINBLASTINE", "A33124I"); lower-case chemistry ("13-cis") does not.
        names = [k for k in self._keys_of(did) if len(k) >= 5]
        orig = "".join(re.findall(r"[A-Za-z0-9]+", _plain(" ".join(tokens[a:b]))))
        if len(orig) != len(key):
            orig = key.upper()                   # cannot align: treat as marked
        for i in range(2, min(6, len(key))):
            for lab, tail, at in ((key[:i], key[i:], i - 1), (key[-i:], key[:-i], len(key) - 1)):
                if not tail or not T.is_isotope_label(lab):
                    continue
                elem = re.sub(r"^\d+m?", "", lab)
                if len(elem) == 1 and not orig[at].isupper():
                    continue

## M28-B1 — focus-word repair is absent from the label guard (P1)

Exact primary: **`trastuzumab not only radiolabelled but also purified`**. This affirms radiolabelling and adds purification. Both current and previous query modules return **TRASTUZUMAB / CHEMBL1201585**, the complete bare-trastuzumab map (**13 graph trials**) and the complete bare trial/sponsor QA evidence (**12 / 12 rows**). The original CLI independently returns the same complete map and evidence arrays. Every actual refusal stage misses: no BLOCKED resolver component, `_resolver_blocked={}`, `_qualified=False`, `_item_has_isotope=False`, `_item_joined=False`, every `_marked` call returns None, `_whole_name=None`, no public or QA Refused entity. [Stage trace](new_results.json), [strict direct/HTTP assertions](assertions.json), [original CLI capture](../checks/independent_m28_http.json).

`_FOCUS` is declared at `src/query.py:243-244` and used by `_item_joined` at `267`; `_item_has_isotope` at `302-305` still starts negation after every `not`. `_negated_end` at `218-219` sees `only` ending in `ly` and extends that negation onto the following strong label `radiolabelled`. The successful conjugation-focused repair therefore leaves the same affirmative semantics broken in the independent label path. `_marked` at `585-587` receives False, and `Engine.explore` at `src/app.py:88-95` draws the ordinary hit.

Seven supporting complete affirmative inputs reproduce the same strict all-stage leak: `trastuzumab not merely radiolabelled but also purified`; `trastuzumab not solely radiolabelled but also purified`; `trastuzumab not only labelled with Alexa Fluor 488 but also purified`; `trastuzumab not merely labelled with Alexa Fluor 488 but also purified`; `trastuzumab not only tagged with biotin but also purified`; `trastuzumab not simply tagged with biotin but also purified`; `trastuzumab not only radioactive but also purified`.

Controls isolate semantics: `trastuzumab only radiolabelled` correctly refuses; true negatives `trastuzumab not radiolabelled` and `trastuzumab not labelled with Alexa Fluor 488` retain TRASTUZUMAB. All seven listed focus words with positive DM1 conjugation are covered by the combined prior/new cases and correctly refuse. Adding only `and flat[i] not in _FOCUS` to the label guard **in memory** makes all eight focus-label leaks refuse, preserves the genuine negatives and ordinary controls, and leaves the dye-code and ADC-exemption leaks unchanged. The helper and caches are restored. [Exact original/replacement and causal outputs](counterfactual.json), [diagnostic script](counterfactual.py).

Identity premise does not require inferring any particular radiopharmaceutical. The affirmative input describes a labelled version and must not silently become the unlabelled drug. For the fluorescent corroborations, the manufacturer's description explicitly explains covalent attachment of Alexa Fluor dye or biotin to IgG. [Thermo Fisher antibody labeling](https://www.thermofisher.com/us/en/home/life-science/antibodies/antibody-labeling.html).

## M28-B2 — standard cyanine and rhodamine abbreviations evade the named-dye guard (P1)

Both current and previous versions draw the complete bare TRASTUZUMAB map and **12 / 12** QA arrays. The same strict all-stage assertions as B1 pass, including `_item_joined=False`. **`trastuzumab linked to BODIPY`** is a further named-dye-family corroboration; the blocker does not depend on resolving a particular BODIPY derivative. [Stage outputs](new_results.json), [equality assertions](assertions.json).

At `src/query.py:240-242`, the code list recognizes `cy5`, `cy7`, `cy55` but omits `cy3` and `tamra`. New `_REAGENTS` entries at `248-251` recognize strings starting `cyanin` or `rhodamin`; ordinary abbreviations have neither prefix. The pieces/reagent predicate at `263-265` is therefore False, and the `linked` branch at `280-284` misses. The literal controls `trastuzumab linked to cyanine 3`, `trastuzumab linked to rhodamine`, `trastuzumab linked to Cy5.5`, and both Alexa cases refuse.

The identity premise is manufacturer-supported, not inferred from an arbitrary capitalized token: Cytiva calls Cy3 a fluorescent cyanine and provides an antibody-conjugation protocol. Thermo Fisher identifies TAMRA as a tetramethylrhodamine derivative used for protein conjugates. [Cytiva Cy3 specification](https://d3.cytivalifesciences.com/prod/IFU/28951573.pdf), [Thermo Fisher rhodamine reference](https://www.thermofisher.com/us/en/home/references/molecular-probes-the-handbook/fluorophores-and-their-amine-reactive-derivatives/long-wavelength-rhodamines-texas-red-dyes-and-qsy-quenchers.html). These sources establish dye identity and the modification interpretation; they do not establish a unique registered trastuzumab-dye product.

Adding only `cy3`, `tamra`, and `bodipy` to the existing code set in memory makes the three dye cases refuse, retains nine ordinary/negative/bare-ADC controls, and leaves the label-focus and ADC-exemption defects intact. This diagnoses vocabulary coverage; it is not a proposed complete dye dictionary. [Causal outputs](counterfactual.json).

## M28-B3 — a recognized extra dye or carrier is ignored for an existing ADC (P1)

Exact primary: **`trastuzumab emtansine linked to Alexa Fluor 488`**. The current map and both complete QA evidence arrays equal **bare trastuzumab emtansine / CHEMBL1743082**, with **5 graph trials / 5 trial QA / 5 sponsor QA rows**. **`trastuzumab deruxtecan linked to Alexa Fluor 488`** similarly equals **CHEMBL4297844**, with **6 / 6 / 4** rows. **`trastuzumab emtansine conjugated to polyethylene glycol`** independently corroborates the same exemption. These are deliberately separated from the 11 cases whose recognizers all return False: here `_item_joined=True`, including when called only on the text outside the matched ADC name. Nevertheless **every actual refusal stage still misses**: resolver unblocked, `_marked=None`, `_whole_name=None`, public linker and QA contain no Refused object, and the map draws the existing ADC. A True internal cue is not a refusal.

`_payload_name` at `src/query.py:234-236` returns True solely because the stored preferred name contains a later `emtansine` or `deruxtecan` word. The conjunction at `590-592` then suppresses the already-detected joining cue outside that matched name. The guard exempts the existing ADC from any additional linkage. This is broader than accepting an already stored ADC name: the new Alexa label is not part of either stored ADC name. The same manufacturer antibody-label chemistry above supports distinguishing a further dye-labelled antibody from the corresponding unlabelled ADC; no unique approved or measured clinical product is asserted.

In memory, replacing only `_payload_name` with `lambda name: False` makes these three extra-modification descriptions refuse while preserving the two bare ADC names in this measured cohort. It leaves B1 and B2 intact. This isolates the broad exemption; removing it globally is **not** offered as a completeness-tested production repair. An eventual repair must still preserve authentic stored ADC names and aliases. [Literal original helper, diagnostic replacement, independent controls and restoration](counterfactual.json).

# Specification review — HEAD^ to HEAD

The change is appropriately scoped to payload-token normalization, named dyes and affirmative focus words, with one regression test. No unrelated scope creep was found in `git diff HEAD^...HEAD -- src/query.py tests/test_app.py`.

The exact M27 repairs work in fresh direct and CLI HTTP evidence: forward/reverse SN-38, DM-1, Alexa 488/647 and complete `not merely/solely/exclusively ... but also ...` conjugation refuse. The genuine negative `not directly conjugated` and ordinary clinical-relation controls retain their drugs. Glued neighbouring pairs implement the stated spelling repair.

Three requirements remain incomplete:

All three are inherited residual defects, not new regressions in this commit. The guard at `src/query.py:590-591` constructs a cache key from the entire list item minus each matched span. With **`' '.join(f'isa x{i:04d}' for i in range(400))`** (3,999 characters, one list item), each removal yields a different tuple. The separate direct instrumented pass records **400 misses / 319,600 words / 1,278,800 aggregate characters** processed by `_item_joined`; its wrapped guard time is 0.37799 s out of 1.04880 s total. At 50 matches / 499 characters it records 50 misses / 4,950 words. These counts demonstrate growing rescan work, not merely host timing variation. [Instrumentation](contract/cost/joined_instrumentation.json).

For the same 3,999-character workload, the ABBA workers retain the same accepted entity in HEAD and HEAD^. HEAD / HEAD^ medians-of-worker-medians are **0.76389 / 0.67499 s**, and first-call cold medians are **1.14445 / 1.13367 s**. The original CLI HTTP check completes in **1.63036 s**, queues an EGFR map for **1.54216 s**, and a warm repeat completes in **1.52518 s**, all 200. [ABBA samples](contract/cost/joined_cost.json), [HTTP](contract/cost/isolated_joined_unique_cost_http.json). This is a **should-fix cost observation**, with no timeout or incorrect map proven by the performance tests. The same ABBA suite covers 499-character matched inputs, repeated conjugation descriptions, a conjugation after many aliases, and negated-adverb text; each completes under the existing character caps. Changing acceptance to refusal is recorded where expected and is not presented as a same-semantics speed comparison.
